# 🇪🇸 Basic Español Complete Master Curriculum (Lessons 1 to 31)

> Authoritative pedagogical specification, master grammar guide, vocabulary reference, and interactive sentence builder database for Lessons 1 through 31.

# 📚 Part 1: Basic Elements & Simple Sentences

> **Part 1 Overview**: Master pure Spanish vowel pronunciation, noun gender (el/la), subject pronouns, and essential regular -AR verb conjugations.

---

### 📖 Lesson 1: Intro to Spanish & Vowel Pronunciation

**Subtitle**: Vowels, Greetings, and Polite Address  
**Professor's Note**: *"Welcome to Spanish! Unlike English where vowels have multiple shifting sounds, Spanish has exactly 5 pure, consistent vowel sounds."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Master pure pronunciation of Spanish vowels (A, E, I, O, U)
- Learn time-appropriate greetings and polite farewells
- Distinguish informal (tú) vs formal (usted) address

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### The 5 Pure Spanish Vowels

Spanish vowels never glide into diphthongs like English vowels. Keep your jaw steady when pronouncing them.

| Vowel | Phonetic Sound | English Keyword Equivalent | Spanish Example Words |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A | ah | like "a" in father | casa (house), cantar (to sing) |
| E | eh | like "e" in met | mesa (table), el (the) |
| I | ee | like "ee" in machine | isla (island), libro (book) |
| O | oh | like "o" in door (pure) | sol (sun), gato (cat) |
| U | oo | like "oo" in boot | uno (one), luna (moon) |

> [!NOTE]
> Tip: Vowels ending words are crisp and short. Never add an "w" or "y" off-glide to Spanish "o" or "e".

##### Greetings & Formal Register (Tú vs Usted)

Greeting someone depends on the time of day and your social relationship.

- Buenos días — Used from sunrise until noon (12:00 PM).
- Buenas tardes — Used from noon (12:00 PM) until sunset/darkness.
- Buenas noches — Used after dark as both a greeting and goodnight.
- ¿Cómo estás? (Tú) — Informal. Use with peers, children, friends, and family.
- ¿Cómo está usted? (Usted) — Formal. Use with professors, elders, strangers, and superiors.

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **¡Hola!** | `OH-lah` | Hello! / Hi! | Universal friendly greeting |
| **Buenos días** | `BWEH-nohs DEE-ahs` | Good morning | Used before noon |
| **Buenas tardes** | `BWEH-nahs TAR-dehs` | Good afternoon | Used between 12 PM and dark |
| **Buenas noches** | `BWEH-nahs NOH-chehs` | Good evening / Good night | Used after dark |
| **¿Cómo te llamas?** | `KOH-moh teh YAH-mahs` | What is your name? (informal) | Asking a peer |
| **Me llamo...** | `Meh YAH-moh` | My name is... | Stating your name |
| **Mucho gusto** | `MOO-choh GOOS-toh` | Nice to meet you | Polite initial reply |
| **Hasta luego** | `AHS-tah LWEH-goh` | See you later | Most common farewell |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `¡Hola! Buenos días, ¿cómo está usted?`  
   **English**: Hello! Good morning, how are you (formal)?  
   *Breakdown*: Formal greeting to a professor or elder.

2. **Spanish**: `Me llamo Elena. Mucho gusto en conocerte.`  
   **English**: My name is Elena. Nice to meet you.  
   *Breakdown*: Standard introduction pairing.

3. **Spanish**: `Hasta mañana, nos vemos en la escuela.`  
   **English**: See you tomorrow, we see each other at school.  
   *Breakdown*: Friendly goodbye between students.

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Profesor Worden**: "¡Buenos días, estudiante! ¿Cómo estás?" (*Good morning, student! How are you?*)
- **Estudiante**: "Buenos días, profesor. Muy bien, gracias. ¿Y usted?" (*Good morning, professor. Very well, thank you. And you?*)
- **Profesor Worden**: "Excelente, gracias. ¡Mucho gusto!" (*Excellent, thank you. Pleased to meet you!*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: How do you politely ask a professor for their name in Spanish?  
**Options**: ¿Cómo te llamas?, ¿Cómo se llama usted?, ¿Qué tal bro?, ¿Quién es tú?  
**Correct Answer**: `¿Cómo se llama usted?`  
**Explanation**: Usted is used for formal address with professors and authority figures.

### 📖 Lesson 2: Nouns & Definite Articles

**Subtitle**: Grammatical Gender, Singular & Plural Articles (El, La, Los, Las)  
**Professor's Note**: *"Every noun in Spanish has a grammatical gender (masculine or feminine). It does not mean objects are male or female, but they belong to grammatical agreements."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Understand masculine (-o) and feminine (-a, -ción, -dad) noun patterns
- Master definite articles: EL, LA, LOS, LAS
- Learn key masculine/feminine exception nouns (el mapa, la mano, el agua)

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### Definite Articles & Gender Concordance

Articles must match both the gender and number of the noun they describe.

| Number | Masculine Article | Feminine Article | Examples |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Singular ("The") | EL | LA | el libro (the book) / la casa (the house) |
| Plural ("The") | LOS | LAS | los libros (the books) / las casas (the houses) |

##### Noun Endings & Major Exceptions

While -o is usually masculine and -a feminine, memorize these crucial exceptions from the syllabus workbook.

- Nouns ending in -o are typically masculine: el chico, el gato, el carro.
- Nouns ending in -a, -ción, -sión, -dad, -tad are feminine: la casa, la lección, la ciudad, la libertad.
- EXCEPTION 1 (Greek origin -ma/-pa endings): el mapa (the map), el problema (the problem), el tema (the topic), el día (the day).
- EXCEPTION 2 (Shortened forms): la mano (hand), la foto (fotografía), la moto (motocicleta).
- STRESSED "A" RULE: Feminine singular nouns starting with stressed "a" use EL in singular to prevent awkward sound, but remain feminine in plural: el agua -> las aguas, el hacha -> las hachas.

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **el libro** | `ehl LEE-broh` | the book | Masculine singular |
| **la mesa** | `lah MEH-sah` | the table | Feminine singular |
| **el mapa** | `ehl MAH-pah` | the map | Masculine exception (-a ending) |
| **la mano** | `lah MAH-noh` | the hand | Feminine exception (-o ending) |
| **el agua** | `ehl AH-gwah` | the water | Stressed "a" feminine noun |
| **la lección** | `lah lehks-YOHN` | the lesson | Feminine ending in -ción |
| **los libros** | `lohs LEE-brohs` | the books | Masculine plural |
| **las casas** | `lahs KAH-sahs` | the houses | Feminine plural |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `El mapa está en la mesa.`  
   **English**: The map is on the table.  
   *Breakdown*: Uses masculine "el mapa" and feminine "la mesa".

2. **Spanish**: `Las universidades son grandes.`  
   **English**: The universities are large.  
   *Breakdown*: Plural feminine agreement: las universidades.

3. **Spanish**: `El agua fría es buena.`  
   **English**: The cold water is good.  
   *Breakdown*: El agua uses "el" singular, but adjective "fría" remains feminine!

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Estudiante 1**: "¿Tienes el mapa de la ciudad?" (*Do you have the map of the city?*)
- **Estudiante 2**: "Sí, el mapa y las lecciones están en el libro." (*Yes, the map and the lessons are in the book.*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: What is the correct singular article for "problema"?  
**Options**: la problema, el problema, las problema, los problema  
**Correct Answer**: `el problema`  
**Explanation**: Nouns ending in Greek -ma (problema, tema, sistema) are masculine exceptions taking "el".

### 📖 Lesson 3: Subject Pronouns & Verb SER

**Subtitle**: Conjugating SER (Identity, Origin & Characteristics)  
**Professor's Note**: *"SER is one of Spanish's most essential irregular verbs. It defines who you are, where you are from, and your inherent traits."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Learn all 12 Spanish subject pronouns
- Master the full present tense conjugation of SER (soy, eres, es, somos, sois, son)
- Apply the DOCTOR acronym for when to use SER

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### Spanish Subject Pronouns Table

Subject pronouns replace names. Note that Spanish often omits the pronoun because verb endings clarify who is speaking.

| Person | Singular | Plural |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1st Person | Yo (I) | Nosotros / Nosotras (We) |
| 2nd Person (Informal) | Tú (You) | Vosotros / Vosotras (You all - Spain) |
| 2nd Person (Formal) | Usted / Ud. (You) | Ustedes / Uds. (You all - LatAm & Spain) |
| 3rd Person | Él (He) / Ella (She) | Ellos (They m.) / Ellas (They f.) |

##### Full Conjugation Matrix of SER

SER is irregular. You must memorize these forms by heart.

| Pronoun | SER Form | English Meaning | Example Sentence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Yo | soy | I am | Yo soy estudiante. |
| Tú | eres | You are (informal) | Tú eres inteligente. |
| Él / Ella / Ud. | es | He/She/You formal is | Ella es profesora. |
| Nosotros/as | somos | We are | Nosotros somos amigos. |
| Vosotros/as | sois | You all are (Spain) | Vosotros sois de Madrid. |
| Ellos / Ellas / Uds. | son | They / You all are | Ellos son de México. |

##### The D.O.C.T.O.R. Rule for SER

Use SER when describing Permanent or Essential attributes:

**D.O.C.T.O.R.**
- **D**: Description — *Example*: Él es alto y simpático.
- **O**: Occupation — *Example*: Soy médico en el hospital.
- **C**: Characteristic — *Example*: El perro es inteligente.
- **T**: Time & Date — *Example*: Hoy es lunes, son las dos.
- **O**: Origin — *Example*: Nosotros somos de España.
- **R**: Relationship — *Example*: María es mi hermana.

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **estudiante** | `ehs-too-DYAHN-teh` | student | Occupation/Role |
| **profesor / profesora** | `proh-feh-SOHR` | teacher / professor | Occupation |
| **amigo / amiga** | `ah-MEE-goh` | friend | Relationship |
| **de** | `deh` | from / of | Origin preposition |
| **inteligente** | `een-teh-lee-HEHN-teh` | intelligent | Description |
| **simpático / a** | `seem-PAH-tee-koh` | nice / friendly | Personality trait |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `Tú y yo somos estudiantes de español.`  
   **English**: You and I are Spanish students.  
   *Breakdown*: Tú y yo = nosotros -> somos.

2. **Spanish**: `¿De dónde eres tú? Yo soy de los Estados Unidos.`  
   **English**: Where are you from? I am from the United States.  
   *Breakdown*: Origin question with ser.

3. **Spanish**: `Ellas son mis hermanas.`  
   **English**: They are my sisters.  
   *Breakdown*: Relationship classification uses son.

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Ana**: "¡Hola! ¿Eres tú el nuevo estudiante?" (*Hi! Are you the new student?*)
- **Mateo**: "Sí, yo soy Mateo. Soy de Colombia. ¿Y tú?" (*Yes, I am Mateo. I am from Colombia. And you?*)
- **Ana**: "Yo soy Ana, soy de Madrid. ¡Mucho gusto!" (*I am Ana, I am from Madrid. Nice to meet you!*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: Which form of SER completes: "Mis padres ___ de México"?  
**Options**: es, somos, son, sois  
**Correct Answer**: `son`  
**Explanation**: "Mis padres" (my parents) corresponds to "ellos" (they), taking "son".

### 📖 Lesson 4: Regular -AR Verbs in Present Tense

**Subtitle**: Conjugating Action Verbs (Hablar, Estudiar, Trabajar)  
**Professor's Note**: *"Verbs are the engine of Spanish! -AR verbs are the largest verb family in Spanish. Master the endings to speak hundreds of sentences."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Drop the -AR infinitive ending to find the verb root
- Attach present tense endings: -o, -as, -a, -amos, -áis, -an
- Build negative sentences using NO before the verb

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### Present Tense -AR Verb Endings

To conjugate any regular -AR verb, drop the "-ar" from the infinitive (e.g. hablar -> habl-) and add the ending matching the subject.

| Subject | Ending | Hablar (to speak) | Estudiar (to study) | Trabajar (to work) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Yo | -o | hablo | estudio | trabajo |
| Tú | -as | hablas | estudias | trabajas |
| Él / Ella / Ud. | -a | habla | estudia | trabaja |
| Nosotros/as | -amos | hablamos | estudiamos | trabajamos |
| Vosotros/as | -áis | habláis | estudiáis | trabajáis |
| Ellos / Ellas / Uds. | -an | hablan | estudian | trabajan |

##### Forming Negative Sentences

To make a sentence negative in Spanish, place "NO" directly BEFORE the conjugated verb.

- Positive: Yo hablo español. (I speak Spanish.)
- Negative: Yo NO hablo francés. (I do not speak French.)
- Question: ¿Estudias tú mucho? (Do you study a lot?)
- Negative Answer: No, no estudio mucho. (No, I do not study a lot.)

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **hablar** | `ah-BLAHR` | to speak / talk | Core -AR verb |
| **estudiar** | `ehs-too-DYAHR` | to study | Core -AR verb |
| **trabajar** | `trah-bah-HAHR` | to work | Core -AR verb |
| **buscar** | `boos-KAHR` | to search / look for | Direct object without preposition |
| **necesitar** | `neh-seh-see-TAHR` | to need | Needs infinitive or noun |
| **comprar** | `kohm-PRAHR` | to buy | Shopping verb |
| **escuchar** | `ehs-koo-CHAHR` | to listen to | Includes "to" |
| **practicar** | `prahk-tee-KAHR` | to practice | Skill practice |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `Nosotros estudiamos español todos los días.`  
   **English**: We study Spanish every day.  
   *Breakdown*: Estudiar conjugated for nosotros (-amos).

2. **Spanish**: `¿Trabajas tú en un hospital?`  
   **English**: Do you work in a hospital?  
   *Breakdown*: Question format by swapping pronoun and verb.

3. **Spanish**: `No, yo no compro el libro caro.`  
   **English**: No, I do not buy the expensive book.  
   *Breakdown*: Double "no": first answers question, second negates verb.

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **María**: "¿Qué estudias tú en la universidad?" (*What do you study at university?*)
- **Juan**: "Estudio literatura. ¿Y tú? ¿Trabajas?" (*I study literature. And you? Do you work?*)
- **María**: "Sí, trabajo en una biblioteca y hablo español con los clientes." (*Yes, I work in a library and speak Spanish with clients.*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: Conjugate "buscar" for "Ellos":  
**Options**: busco, buscas, busca, buscan  
**Correct Answer**: `buscan`  
**Explanation**: Ellos uses ending "-an": buscan.

# 📚 Part 2: Numbers, Questions, Estar & Ir

> **Part 2 Overview**: Cardinal & ordinal numbers, question formation, locations & temporary states (Estar), and future intention (Ir + a + Infinitive).

---

### 📖 Lesson 5: Indefinite Articles & Numbers 0–100

**Subtitle**: Un, Una, Unos, Unas & Counting in Spanish  
**Professor's Note**: *"Indefinite articles introduce unspecific items ("a book", "some girls"). Pair them with Spanish numbers to quantify everything around you."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Master indefinite articles: UN, UNA, UNOS, UNAS
- Learn numbers 0 to 100 with accurate spelling
- Apply the "tens + y + units" rule for numbers 31–99

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### Indefinite Articles (A, An, Some)

Indefinite articles agree with nouns in gender and number.

| Gender / Number | Singular ("A / An") | Plural ("Some / A few") | Example |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Masculine | UN | UNOS | un chico (a boy) / unos chicos (some boys) |
| Feminine | UNA | UNAS | una manzana (an apple) / unas manzanas (some apples) |

##### Numbers 0 to 100 Construction Rules

Learn the base tens and combining formulas.

- 0-15: Unique words: cero, uno, dos, tres, cuatro, cinco, seis, siete, ocho, nueve, diez, once, doce, trece, catorce, quince.
- 16-29: Combined single words: dieciséis, diecisiete, dieciocho, diecinueve, veinte, veintiuno, veintidós, veintitrés... veintinueve.
- 30-90 (Tens): treinta, cuarenta, cincuenta, sesenta, setenta, ochenta, noventa, cien.
- 31-99 Formula: Three separate words: [Ten] + Y + [Unit] (e.g. 35 = treinta y cinco, 74 = setenta y cuatro).

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **un / una** | `oon / OO-nah` | a / an | Singular indefinite article |
| **unos / unas** | `OO-nohs / OO-nahs` | some / a few | Plural indefinite article |
| **diez** | `DYEHS` | ten (10) | Number |
| **veinte** | `VAYN-teh` | twenty (20) | Number |
| **treinta** | `TRAYN-tah` | thirty (30) | Number |
| **cuarenta** | `kwah-REHN-tah` | forty (40) | Number |
| **cincuenta** | `seen-KWEHN-tah` | fifty (50) | Number |
| **cien** | `SYEHN` | one hundred (100) | Used before nouns or exactly 100 |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `Tengo unos libros y una pluma.`  
   **English**: I have some books and a pen.  
   *Breakdown*: Uses masculine plural "unos" and feminine singular "una".

2. **Spanish**: `Hay cuarenta y cinco estudiantes en la clase.`  
   **English**: There are forty-five students in the class.  
   *Breakdown*: Cuarenta y cinco follows the 31-99 three-word rule.

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Vendedor**: "¿Cuánto cuesta esa camisa?" (*How much does that shirt cost?*)
- **Comprador**: "Cuesta treinta y cinco dólares." (*It costs thirty-five dollars.*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: How do you write "58" in Spanish?  
**Options**: cincuenta y ocho, cincuentaocho, veintiocho, cincuenta u ocho  
**Correct Answer**: `cincuenta y ocho`  
**Explanation**: Numbers 31-99 use 3 separate words: tens + y + units.

### 📖 Lesson 6: Verb ESTAR & Numbers Over 100

**Subtitle**: Conjugating ESTAR, PLACE Acronym & Counting >100  
**Professor's Note**: *"Unlike SER (permanent nature), ESTAR expresses temporary conditions, emotional states, and physical locations."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Master the conjugation of ESTAR (estoy, estás, está, estamos, estáis, están)
- Apply the P.L.A.C.E. mnemonic for when to use ESTAR
- Learn numbers above 100 (ciento, quinientos, mil)

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### Conjugation Table of ESTAR

Note the accent marks on estás, está, estáis, and están!

| Subject | ESTAR Form | English | Example |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Yo | estoy | I am | Yo estoy en casa. |
| Tú | estás | You are | ¿Dónde estás tú? |
| Él / Ella / Ud. | está | He/She/You formal is | Él está cansado. |
| Nosotros/as | estamos | We are | Estamos listos. |
| Vosotros/as | estáis | You all are | Vosotros estáis aquí. |
| Ellos / Ellas / Uds. | están | They / You all are | Ellos están felices. |

##### The P.L.A.C.E. Rule for ESTAR

**P.L.A.C.E.**
- **P**: Position — *Example*: El libro está en la mesa.
- **L**: Location — *Example*: Madrid está en España.
- **A**: Action (Progressive) — *Example*: Estoy estudiando ahora.
- **C**: Condition — *Example*: La puerta está cerrada.
- **E**: Emotion — *Example*: Ella está contenta hoy.

##### Numbers Over 100 Reference

Notice irregular hundred forms like 500 (quinientos), 700 (setecientos), 900 (novecientos).

- 100 exact = cien. 101-199 = ciento uno, ciento dos...
- 200 = doscientos / doscientas
- 500 = quinientos / quinientas (IRREGULAR)
- 700 = setecientos / setecientas (IRREGULAR)
- 900 = novecientos / novecientas (IRREGULAR)
- 1,000 = mil. 2,000 = dos mil.

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **estoy** | `ehs-TOY` | I am (location/state) | ESTAR 1st person |
| **estás** | `ehs-TAHS` | you are (location/state) | ESTAR 2nd person |
| **cansado / a** | `kahn-SAH-doh` | tired | Condition (Estar) |
| **ocupado / a** | `oh-koo-PAH-doh` | busy | Condition (Estar) |
| **feliz / contento** | `feh-LEES` | happy / content | Emotion (Estar) |
| **quinientos** | `kee-NYEHN-tohs` | five hundred (500) | Irregular hundred |
| **mil** | `meel` | one thousand (1,000) | Number |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `¿Dónde están Uds.? Estamos en la biblioteca.`  
   **English**: Where are you all? We are at the library.  
   *Breakdown*: Location rule uses estar.

2. **Spanish**: `El hotel cuesta quinientos dólares.`  
   **English**: The hotel costs five hundred dollars.  
   *Breakdown*: Quinientos = 500.

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Carlos**: "¡Hola Laura! ¿Cómo estás hoy?" (*Hi Laura! How are you today?*)
- **Laura**: "Estoy muy ocupada pero feliz. ¿Y tú?" (*I am very busy but happy. And you?*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: Which sentence correctly describes temporary emotion?  
**Options**: Ella es triste hoy., Ella está triste hoy., Ella somos triste hoy., Ella estar triste hoy.  
**Correct Answer**: `Ella está triste hoy.`  
**Explanation**: Emotions are temporary states governed by ESTAR.

| 1 | **Yo estoy contento en la escuela** | I am happy at school | `Yo (Subject) + estoy (Verb) + contento (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + escuela (Place)` | Uses 'estar' for temporary emotional state combined with location preposition 'en'. |
| 2 | **Tú estás muy cansado en casa** | You are very tired at home | `Tú (Subject) + estás (Verb) + muy (Object) + cansado (Object) + en (Place) + casa (Place)` | Demonstrates 'estás' with physical state adjective 'cansado' and location 'en casa'. |

| 33 | **María tiene trescientas cartas en el escritorio** | María has three hundred letters on the desk | `María (Subject) + tiene (Verb) + trescientas (Object) + cartas (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + escritorio (Place)` | Shows feminine agreement with 'trescientas' modifying feminine noun 'cartas'. |
| 34 | **Nosotros vendemos cuatrocientos boletos en la escuela** | We sell four hundred tickets at the school | `Nosotros (Subject) + vendemos (Verb) + cuatrocientos (Object) + boletos (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + escuela (Place)` | Demonstrates SVO layout with 'cuatrocientos' and 'en la escuela' location. |
| 35 | **Yo guardo quinientos dólares en casa** | I keep five hundred dollars at home | `Yo (Subject) + guardo (Verb) + quinientos (Object) + dólares (Object) + en (Place) + casa (Place)` | Uses 'quinientos' with standard location expression 'en casa'. |
| 36 | **Tú cuentas ochocientos coches en el centro** | You count eight hundred cars downtown | `Tú (Subject) + cuentas (Verb) + ochocientos (Object) + coches (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + centro (Place)` | Features 'ochocientos' modifying an object with location phrase 'en el centro'. |
| 37 | **El museo está en el kilómetro cuatrocientos** | The museum is at kilometer four hundred | `El (Subject) + museo (Subject) + está (Verb) + en (Place) + el (Place) + kilómetro (Place) + cuatrocientos (Place)` | Uses 'está' for physical location along with 'cuatrocientos'. |
| 38 | **Nosotros estamos muy contentos en el hotel** | We are very happy in the hotel | `Nosotros (Subject) + estamos (Verb) + muy (Object) + contentos (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + hotel (Place)` | Combines temporary state 'estamos contentos' with place 'en el hotel'. |
| 39 | **Ella está muy cansada en la escuela** | She is very tired at school | `Ella (Subject) + está (Verb) + muy (Object) + cansada (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + escuela (Place)` | Shows feminine adjective agreement with 'está' and place expression. |
| 40 | **Los setecientos estudiantes están en el gimnasio** | The seven hundred students are in the gym | `Los (Subject) + setecientos (Subject) + estudiantes (Subject) + están (Verb) + en (Place) + el (Place) + gimnasio (Place)` | Uses plural number 'setecientos' in subject noun phrase with 'están'. |

### 📖 Lesson 7: Regular -ER & -IR Verbs in Present

**Subtitle**: Conjugating Comer, Beber, Vivir & Escribir  
**Professor's Note**: *"-ER and -IR verbs share almost identical present tense endings, differing only in the nosotros (-emos vs -imos) and vosotros (-éis vs -ís) forms."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Conjugate regular -ER verbs (-o, -es, -e, -emos, -éis, -en)
- Conjugate regular -IR verbs (-o, -es, -e, -imos, -ís, -en)
- Expand vocabulary with common daily action verbs

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### Side-by-Side Endings Comparison

Compare how -ER and -IR verbs conjugate.

| Subject | -ER Ending (Comer) | -IR Ending (Vivir) | Comer Example | Vivir Example |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Yo | -o | -o | como (I eat) | vivo (I live) |
| Tú | -es | -es | comes (you eat) | vives (you live) |
| Él / Ella / Ud. | -e | -e | come (he eats) | vive (she lives) |
| Nosotros/as | -emos | -imos | comemos (we eat) | vivimos (we live) |
| Vosotros/as | -éis | -ís | coméis (you all eat) | vivís (you all live) |
| Ellos / Ellas / Uds. | -en | -en | comen (they eat) | viven (they live) |

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **comer** | `koh-MEHR` | to eat | Regular -ER verb |
| **beber** | `beh-BEHR` | to drink | Regular -ER verb |
| **aprender** | `ah-prehn-DEHR` | to learn | Regular -ER verb |
| **comprender** | `kohm-prehn-DEHR` | to understand | Regular -ER verb |
| **vivir** | `vee-VEER` | to live | Regular -IR verb |
| **escribir** | `ehs-kree-BEER` | to write | Regular -IR verb |
| **abrir** | `ah-BREER` | to open | Regular -IR verb |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `Nosotros comemos tacos y bebemos agua.`  
   **English**: We eat tacos and drink water.  
   *Breakdown*: Comer (-emos) and beber (-emos).

2. **Spanish**: `¿Dónde vives tú? Vivo en Madrid.`  
   **English**: Where do you live? I live in Madrid.  
   *Breakdown*: Vivir conjugated for tú and yo.

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Pedro**: "¿Comes tú carne?" (*Do you eat meat?*)
- **Sofía**: "No, no como carne. Bebo leche y como frutas." (*No, I don't eat meat. I drink milk and eat fruits.*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: What is the "nosotros" form of "escribir"?  
**Options**: escribemos, escribimos, escriben, escribo  
**Correct Answer**: `escribimos`  
**Explanation**: -IR verbs take "-imos" for nosotros: escribimos.

| 2 | **Tú bebes agua en el parque.** | You drink water in the park. | `Tú (Subject) + bebes (Verb) + agua (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + parque. (Place)` | Demonstrates second-person informal (-es) conjugation of regular -ER verb beber. |
| 3 | **Ella lee un libro en la biblioteca.** | She reads a book in the library. | `Ella (Subject) + lee (Verb) + un (Object) + libro (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + biblioteca. (Place)` | Demonstrates third-person singular (-e) conjugation of regular -ER verb leer. |
| 4 | **Nosotros escribimos una carta en la oficina.** | We write a letter in the office. | `Nosotros (Subject) + escribimos (Verb) + una (Object) + carta (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + oficina. (Place)` | Demonstrates first-person plural (-imos) conjugation of regular -IR verb escribir. |

| 32 | **El hombre vende frutas en el mercado** | The man sells fruit in the market | `El (Subject) + hombre (Subject) + vende (Verb) + frutas (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + mercado (Place)` | Illustrates third-person singular conjugation of regular -ER verb vender with a multi-word noun phrase subject. |
| 33 | **Vosotros bebéis café en la cafetería** | You all drink coffee in the coffee shop | `Vosotros (Subject) + bebéis (Verb) + café (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + cafetería (Place)` | Focuses on second-person plural (vosotros) ending for regular -ER verb beber. |
| 34 | **Tú lees el periódico en el salón** | You read the newspaper in the living room | `Tú (Subject) + lees (Verb) + el (Object) + periódico (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + salón (Place)` | Demonstrates informal second-person singular conjugation of leer. |
| 35 | **Yo escribo notas en la clase** | I write notes in the class | `Yo (Subject) + escribo (Verb) + notas (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + clase (Place)` | Shows first-person singular present tense conjugation for -IR verb escribir. |
| 36 | **Los estudiantes responden preguntas en el aula** | The students answer questions in the classroom | `Los (Subject) + estudiantes (Subject) + responden (Verb) + preguntas (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + aula (Place)` | Demonstrates plural subject noun with third-person plural regular -ER verb responder. |
| 37 | **Ella comprende la lección en la casa** | She understands the lesson in the house | `Ella (Subject) + comprende (Verb) + la (Object) + lección (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + casa (Place)` | Shows third-person singular present tense of regular -ER verb comprender. |
| 38 | **Nosotros comemos pizza en el restaurante** | We eat pizza in the restaurant | `Nosotros (Subject) + comemos (Verb) + pizza (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + restaurante (Place)` | Focuses on first-person plural conjugation of regular -ER verb comer. |
| 39 | **El chico corre diez kilómetros en la playa** | The boy runs ten kilometers on the beach | `El (Subject) + chico (Subject) + corre (Verb) + diez (Object) + kilómetros (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + playa (Place)` | Uses the regular -ER verb correr with a numerical direct object complement. |
| 40 | **Tú abres la puerta en el trabajo** | You open the door at work | `Tú (Subject) + abres (Verb) + la (Object) + puerta (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + trabajo (Place)` | Demonstrates informal second-person singular conjugation for regular -IR verb abrir. |

### 📖 Lesson 8: Irregular Verb IR & Near Future

**Subtitle**: Conjugating IR, Destinations & Question Words  
**Professor's Note**: *"IR (to go) is highly irregular. Combined with "a + infinitive", it unlocks the near future tense ("I am going to study")."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Conjugate irregular verb IR (voy, vas, va, vamos, vais, van)
- Form near future sentences using IR + A + INFINITIVE
- Master essential Spanish question words (¿Qué?, ¿Dónde?, ¿Adónde?)

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### Conjugation Matrix of IR (To Go)

IR has no "i" in its present tense forms!

| Subject | IR Conjugation | English | Destination Example |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Yo | voy | I go / am going | Yo voy al parque. |
| Tú | vas | You go | ¿Vas a la clase? |
| Él / Ella / Ud. | va | He / She goes | Ella va al cine. |
| Nosotros/as | vamos | We go | Vamos a la playa. |
| Vosotros/as | vais | You all go | Vosotros vais a casa. |
| Ellos / Ellas / Uds. | van | They go | Ellos van a España. |

> [!NOTE]
> Contraction Rule: A + EL contracts into AL. (e.g. voy a + el parque -> voy al parque).

##### Near Future Formula & Interrogatives Grid

Formula: [Conjugated IR] + A + [Infinitive Verb]

- Voy a comer = I am going to eat.
- Vamos a estudiar = We are going to study.
- ¿Qué? = What?
- ¿Quién? / ¿Quiénes? = Who?
- ¿Dónde? = Where (location)?
- ¿Adónde? = Where to (destination)?
- ¿Cuándo? = When?
- ¿Por qué? = Why? (Answer: Porque... = Because...)

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **voy** | `voy` | I go / am going | IR 1st person |
| **al (a + el)** | `ahl` | to the (masculine singular) | Mandatory contraction |
| **a la** | `ah lah` | to the (feminine singular) | Preposition + article |
| **¿Adónde?** | `ah-DOHN-deh` | Where to? | Destination question |
| **¿Por qué?** | `pohr KEH` | Why? | Question (two words, accent) |
| **porque** | `pohr-keh` | because | Answer (one word, no accent) |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `¿Adónde vas tú? Voy al supermercado.`  
   **English**: Where are you going? I am going to the supermarket.  
   *Breakdown*: Uses ¿Adónde? for destination and contraction "al".

2. **Spanish**: `Esta noche vamos a estudiar para el examen.`  
   **English**: Tonight we are going to study for the exam.  
   *Breakdown*: Near future: vamos + a + estudiar.

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Diego**: "¿Adónde van Uds. este fin de semana?" (*Where are you all going this weekend?*)
- **Lucía**: "Vamos a viajar a México. ¡Vamos a comer tacos!" (*We are going to travel to Mexico. We are going to eat tacos!*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: Which formula correctly expresses "We are going to play"?  
**Options**: Vamos jugar, Vamos a jugar, Va a jugar, Iremos jugar  
**Correct Answer**: `Vamos a jugar`  
**Explanation**: Near future requires IR + A + Infinitive.

#### 🧩 Interactive Sentence Builder Database (Lesson 8: 40 Exercises)

| # | Spanish Sentence | English Translation | Token Breakdown | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **Yo voy a comprar pan al supermercado** | I am going to buy bread at the supermarket | `Yo (Subject) + voy (Verb) + a (Verb) + comprar (Verb) + pan (Object) + al (Place) + supermercado (Place)` | Demonstrates near future (ir + a + infinitive) with first-person singular voy and contracted preposition al. |
| 2 | **Tú vas a estudiar en la biblioteca** | You are going to study in the library | `Tú (Subject) + vas (Verb) + a (Verb) + estudiar (Verb) + en (Place) + la (Place) + biblioteca (Place)` | Uses second-person informal vas + a + infinitive followed by a locational prepositional phrase. |
| 3 | **Ella va a comer en el restaurante** | She is going to eat at the restaurant | `Ella (Subject) + va (Verb) + a (Verb) + comer (Verb) + en (Place) + el (Place) + restaurante (Place)` | Third-person singular va + a + infinitive with place preposition en. |
| 4 | **Nosotros vamos a jugar en el parque** | We are going to play in the park | `Nosotros (Subject) + vamos (Verb) + a (Verb) + jugar (Verb) + en (Place) + el (Place) + parque (Place)` | First-person plural vamos + a + infinitive denoting near-future activity in a location. |
| 5 | **Ellos van a caminar por la playa** | They are going to walk along the beach | `Ellos (Subject) + van (Verb) + a (Verb) + caminar (Verb) + por (Place) + la (Place) + playa (Place)` | Third-person plural van + a + infinitive combined with the preposition por for movement along a place. |
| 6 | **¿Adónde vas tú esta tarde?** | Where are you going this afternoon? | `¿Adónde (Place) + vas (Verb) + tú (Subject) + esta (Time) + tarde? (Time)` | Question asking about destination using ¿Adónde? with the verb ir. |
| 7 | **¿Dónde van a trabajar ustedes hoy?** | Where are you all going to work today? | `¿Dónde (Place) + van (Verb) + a (Verb) + trabajar (Verb) + ustedes (Subject) + hoy? (Time)` | Question with ¿Dónde? combined with near-future structure van a trabajar and subject ustedes. |
| 8 | **¿Cuándo vamos a visitar a la abuela?** | When are we going to visit grandma? | `¿Cuándo (Time) + vamos (Verb) + a (Verb) + visitar (Verb) + a (Object) + la (Object) + abuela? (Object)` | Time question word ¿Cuándo? with near-future construction and personal 'a' for a direct object person. |
| 9 | **¿Por qué va María al hospital?** | Why is María going to the hospital? | `¿Por (Other) + qué (Other) + va (Verb) + María (Subject) + al (Place) + hospital? (Place)` | Asks reason with ¿Por qué? using simple present of ir and contracted preposition al. |
| 10 | **¿Cómo van los niños a la escuela?** | How do the children go to school? | `¿Cómo (Other) + van (Verb) + los (Subject) + niños (Subject) + a (Place) + la (Place) + escuela? (Place)` | Manner question ¿Cómo? with third-person plural van and noun phrase subject los niños. |
| 11 | **Yo voy al gimnasio por la mañana** | I go to the gym in the morning | `Yo (Subject) + voy (Verb) + al (Place) + gimnasio (Place) + por (Time) + la (Time) + mañana (Time)` | Simple present of ir with first-person pronoun, contracted al (a + el), and temporal expression por la mañana. |
| 12 | **Tú vas a leer un libro aquí** | You are going to read a book here | `Tú (Subject) + vas (Verb) + a (Verb) + leer (Verb) + un (Object) + libro (Object) + aquí (Place)` | Near-future construction vas a leer with direct object un libro and location adverb aquí. |
| 13 | **Él va a la piscina hoy** | He is going to the pool today | `Él (Subject) + va (Verb) + a (Place) + la (Place) + piscina (Place) + hoy (Time)` | Simple present destination pattern (ir + a + feminine place) with subject pronoun él. |
| 14 | **Nosotros vamos al cine esta noche** | We are going to the cinema tonight | `Nosotros (Subject) + vamos (Verb) + al (Place) + cine (Place) + esta (Time) + noche (Time)` | First-person plural present ir with destination contraction al and time expression esta noche. |
| 15 | **Ellas van a comer en la cafetería** | They are going to eat in the cafeteria | `Ellas (Subject) + van (Verb) + a (Verb) + comer (Verb) + en (Place) + la (Place) + cafetería (Place)` | Third-person feminine plural near future van a comer with locational phrase en la cafetería. |
| 16 | **¿Qué va a hacer Pedro mañana?** | What is Pedro going to do tomorrow? | `¿Qué (Object) + va (Verb) + a (Verb) + hacer (Verb) + Pedro (Subject) + mañana? (Time)` | Question word ¿Qué? with near future verb structure va a hacer and proper noun subject Pedro. |
| 17 | **¿Adónde va el profesor ahora?** | Where is the teacher going now? | `¿Adónde (Place) + va (Verb) + el (Subject) + profesor (Subject) + ahora? (Time)` | Question with ¿Adónde? asking for destination of subject noun phrase el profesor. |
| 18 | **Yo voy a correr en el parque** | I am going to run in the park | `Yo (Subject) + voy (Verb) + a (Verb) + correr (Verb) + en (Place) + el (Place) + parque (Place)` | First-person singular near future voy a correr with prepositional location phrase en el parque. |
| 19 | **Tú vas a tomar un café allí** | You are going to have a coffee there | `Tú (Subject) + vas (Verb) + a (Verb) + tomar (Verb) + un (Object) + café (Object) + allí (Place)` | Near-future structure with object noun phrase un café and spatial adverb allí. |
| 20 | **Carlos va a la oficina temprano** | Carlos goes to the office early | `Carlos (Subject) + va (Verb) + a (Place) + la (Place) + oficina (Place) + temprano (Time)` | Proper noun subject with verb ir indicating direction (a la oficina) and time adverb temprano. |
| 21 | **Nosotros vamos a viajar a Madrid** | We are going to travel to Madrid | `Nosotros (Subject) + vamos (Verb) + a (Verb) + viajar (Verb) + a (Place) + Madrid (Place)` | Near-future pattern vamos a viajar followed by directional preposition a and proper city name. |
| 22 | **¿Por qué vas a la tienda?** | Why are you going to the store? | `¿Por (Other) + qué (Other) + vas (Verb) + a (Place) + la (Place) + tienda? (Place)` | Question asking for cause using ¿Por qué? with simple present verb vas and feminine destination. |
| 23 | **¿Cuándo va Ana al centro comercial?** | When is Ana going to the shopping mall? | `¿Cuándo (Time) + va (Verb) + Ana (Subject) + al (Place) + centro (Place) + comercial? (Place)` | Time question ¿Cuándo? with verb va, subject Ana, and contracted destination al centro comercial. |
| 24 | **Ustedes van a ver una película** | You all are going to watch a movie | `Ustedes (Subject) + van (Verb) + a (Verb) + ver (Verb) + una (Object) + película (Object)` | Formal plural subject ustedes with near future van a ver and direct object una película. |
| 25 | **Mi hermano va a la universidad** | My brother goes to the university | `Mi (Subject) + hermano (Subject) + va (Verb) + a (Place) + la (Place) + universidad (Place)` | Possessive subject noun phrase Mi hermano with simple present verb va and destination place. |
| 26 | **Yo voy al parque hoy** | I am going to the park today | `Yo (Subject) + voy (Verb) + al (Place) + parque (Place) + hoy (Time)` | Demonstrates simple present tense of 'ir' with contracted preposition 'al' (a + el) and temporal adverb 'hoy'. |
| 27 | **Tú vas a estudiar español** | You are going to study Spanish | `Tú (Subject) + vas (Verb) + a (Verb) + estudiar (Verb) + español (Object)` | Demonstrates near future construction 'ir + a + infinitive' with second person singular 'tú'. |
| 28 | **Ella va a comer pizza** | She is going to eat pizza | `Ella (Subject) + va (Verb) + a (Verb) + comer (Verb) + pizza (Object)` | Demonstrates third person singular near future 'va a' followed by infinitive verb 'comer'. |
| 29 | **Nosotros vamos a la escuela** | We are going to the school | `Nosotros (Subject) + vamos (Verb) + a (Place) + la (Place) + escuela (Place)` | Demonstrates first-person plural 'vamos' with directional preposition 'a' and feminine noun 'la escuela'. |
| 30 | **Ellos van a jugar fútbol** | They are going to play soccer | `Ellos (Subject) + van (Verb) + a (Verb) + jugar (Verb) + fútbol (Object)` | Demonstrates third-person plural near future 'van a' with a direct object sport. |
| 31 | **¿Cuándo vamos a viajar nosotros?** | When are we going to travel? | `¿Cuándo (Time) + vamos (Verb) + a (Verb) + viajar (Verb) + nosotros? (Subject)` | Demonstrates time question word '¿Cuándo?' with near future phrase 'vamos a viajar'. |
| 32 | **¿Por qué vas a la biblioteca?** | Why are you going to the library? | `¿Por (Other) + qué (Other) + vas (Verb) + a (Place) + la (Place) + biblioteca? (Place)` | Demonstrates question word '¿Por qué?' asking for reason combined with verb 'ir' to a place. |
| 33 | **¿Dónde va a trabajar Elena?** | Where is Elena going to work? | `¿Dónde (Place) + va (Verb) + a (Verb) + trabajar (Verb) + Elena? (Subject)` | Demonstrates location question word '¿Dónde?' with near future 'va a trabajar' and named subject. |
| 34 | **El profesor va a leer un libro** | The professor is going to read a book | `El (Subject) + profesor (Subject) + va (Verb) + a (Verb) + leer (Verb) + un (Object) + libro (Object)` | Demonstrates standard SVO order using noun phrase subject and near future action. |
| 35 | **Yo voy a preparar un café** | I am going to prepare a coffee | `Yo (Subject) + voy (Verb) + a (Verb) + preparar (Verb) + un (Object) + café (Object)` | Demonstrates 'yo voy a' near future structure with transitive infinitive 'preparar'. |
| 36 | **¿Cómo van ustedes al museo?** | How are you all going to the museum? | `¿Cómo (Other) + van (Verb) + ustedes (Subject) + al (Place) + museo? (Place)` | Demonstrates question word '¿Cómo?' asking for manner/means of transportation with plural 'ustedes'. |
| 37 | **Mi hermana va al centro comercial** | My sister goes to the shopping mall | `Mi (Subject) + hermana (Subject) + va (Verb) + al (Place) + centro (Place) + comercial (Place)` | Demonstrates subject possessive 'Mi hermana' with verb 'va' and contracted preposition 'al'. |
| 38 | **Nosotros vamos a visitar el museo** | We are going to visit the museum | `Nosotros (Subject) + vamos (Verb) + a (Verb) + visitar (Verb) + el (Object) + museo (Object)` | Demonstrates first-person plural near future 'vamos a' followed by infinitive 'visitar'. |
| 39 | **¿Cuándo vas a comprar la fruta?** | When are you going to buy the fruit? | `¿Cuándo (Time) + vas (Verb) + a (Verb) + comprar (Verb) + la (Object) + fruta? (Object)` | Demonstrates question word '¿Cuándo?' paired with near future verb 'vas a comprar'. |
| 40 | **Tú vas a escribir una carta** | You are going to write a letter | `Tú (Subject) + vas (Verb) + a (Verb) + escribir (Verb) + una (Object) + carta (Object)` | Demonstrates 'ir + a + infinitive' with second-person singular pronoun and direct object. |

# 📚 Part 3: Dates, Time, Tener & Hacer

> **Part 3 Overview**: Days, months, telling time, possession & obligation (Tener / Tener que), and weather & idiomatic expressions (Hacer).

---

### 📖 Lesson 9: Days, Months, Seasons & Date Format

**Subtitle**: Calendar Vocabulary & Date Syntax  
**Professor's Note**: *"Dates in Spanish follow a logical numeric structure: "the [day] of [month]". Months and days are NOT capitalized in Spanish unless starting a sentence."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Learn days of the week, months, and 4 seasons
- Format dates accurately using: Es el [day] de [month]
- Understand capitalization rules for calendar terms

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### Days, Months & Seasons Reference Table

All days of the week are masculine in Spanish (el lunes, los martes).

| Days of Week | Months of Year | Seasons |
| --- | --- | --- |
| lunes (Monday) | enero, febrero, marzo | la primavera (Spring) |
| martes (Tuesday) | abril, mayo, junio | el verano (Summer) |
| miércoles (Wednesday) | julio, agosto, septiembre | el otoño (Autumn) |
| jueves (Thursday) | octubre, noviembre | el invierno (Winter) |
| viernes (Friday) | diciembre | - |
| sábado, domingo | - | - |

##### Date Construction Syntax

Formula: [Es el] + [number] + [de] + [month]

- Today is May 5th -> Hoy es el cinco de mayo.
- The first of the month uses "el primero": El primero de enero (New Year's Day).
- Notice months and days are written in lowercase: mayo, lunes.

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **el lunes** | `ehl LOO-nehs` | Monday | Day of week |
| **el viernes** | `ehl VYEHR-nehs` | Friday | Day of week |
| **enero** | `eh-NEH-roh` | January | Month |
| **mayo** | `MAH-yoh` | May | Month |
| **el verano** | `ehl veh-RAH-noh` | summer | Season |
| **la primavera** | `lah pree-mah-VEH-rah` | spring | Season |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `Hoy es viernes, el cuatro de julio.`  
   **English**: Today is Friday, the fourth of July.  
   *Breakdown*: Full date structure.

2. **Spanish**: `En el invierno hace mucho frío.`  
   **English**: In the winter it is very cold.  
   *Breakdown*: Combines season with weather expression.

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Estudiante**: "¿Cuál es la fecha de hoy?" (*What is today's date?*)
- **Profesor**: "Hoy es el quince de octubre." (*Today is the fifteenth of October.*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: How do you state "December 25th" in Spanish?  
**Options**: el 25 de Diciembre, el veinticinco de diciembre, el diciembre 25, el 25 en diciembre  
**Correct Answer**: `el veinticinco de diciembre`  
**Explanation**: Dates use el + number + de + lowercase month.

| 2 | **Yo compro el pan en la tienda los martes** | I buy bread at the store on Tuesdays | `Yo (Subject) + compro (Verb) + el (Object) + pan (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + tienda (Place) + los (Time) + martes (Time)` | Demonstrates SVO + Place + Time order with habitual day expression |
| 3 | **Nosotros jugamos un partido en el parque el jueves** | We play a game in the park on Thursday | `Nosotros (Subject) + jugamos (Verb) + un (Object) + partido (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + parque (Place) + el (Time) + jueves (Time)` | Uses singular article 'el' for a specific upcoming day |
| 4 | **Tú lees un libro en la terraza los viernes** | You read a book on the terrace on Fridays | `Tú (Subject) + lees (Verb) + un (Object) + libro (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + terraza (Place) + los (Time) + viernes (Time)` | Habitual weekly event using 'los viernes' |

| 16 | **Yo preparo la maleta en mi casa en octubre** | I pack the suitcase at my house in October | `Yo (Subject) + preparo (Verb) + la (Object) + maleta (Object) + en (Place) + mi (Place) + casa (Place) + en (Time) + octubre (Time)` | First person singular present tense with month 'octubre' |
| 17 | **Ella compra fruta en el mercado en noviembre** | She buys fruit at the market in November | `Ella (Subject) + compra (Verb) + fruta (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + mercado (Place) + en (Time) + noviembre (Time)` | Standard SVO + Place + Time pattern with 'noviembre' |
| 18 | **Nosotros organizamos la cena en el salón en diciembre** | We organize dinner in the living room in December | `Nosotros (Subject) + organizamos (Verb) + la (Object) + cena (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + salón (Place) + en (Time) + diciembre (Time)` | Winter month 'diciembre' used in time phrase |
| 19 | **Tú escuchas música en tu habitación en primavera** | You listen to music in your room in spring | `Tú (Subject) + escuchas (Verb) + música (Object) + en (Place) + tu (Place) + habitación (Place) + en (Time) + primavera (Time)` | Season expression 'en primavera' denoting time |

### 📖 Lesson 10: Telling Time in Spanish

**Subtitle**: Es la una vs Son las..., Minutes & Time of Day  
**Professor's Note**: *"Telling time uses the verb SER. Use "Es la una" for 1:00 (singular) and "Son las..." for all other hours (2 through 12)."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Master time questions: ¿Qué hora es? vs ¿A qué hora es...?
- Add minutes using "y" and subtract using "menos"
- Specify time of day: de la mañana, de la tarde, de la noche

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### Telling Time Rules & Structure

Time uses feminine agreement matching "la hora" (the hour).

| Time Category | Spanish Expression | English Equivalent |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1:00 (Singular) | Es la una. | It is 1:00. |
| 2:00-12:00 (Plural) | Son las dos / Son las tres... | It is 2:00 / It is 3:00... |
| Quarter past (:15) | Son las cuatro y cuarto. | It is 4:15. |
| Half past (:30) | Son las cinco y media. | It is 5:30. |
| Minutes to next hour | Son las seis menos diez. | It is 5:50 (ten to six). |

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **¿Qué hora es?** | `keh OH-rah ehs` | What time is it? | Current time question |
| **y cuarto** | `ee KWAHR-toh` | quarter past (:15) | Adding 15 mins |
| **y media** | `ee MEH-dyah` | half past (:30) | Adding 30 mins |
| **menos** | `MEH-nohs` | minus / to (minutes) | Subtracting minutes |
| **de la mañana** | `deh lah mah-NYAH-nah` | in the morning (with time) | AM indicator |
| **de la noche** | `deh lah NOH-cheh` | at night (with time) | PM indicator |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `Es la una y diez de la tarde.`  
   **English**: It is 1:10 in the afternoon.  
   *Breakdown*: Singular es la una.

2. **Spanish**: `La clase es a las ocho y media.`  
   **English**: The class is at eight thirty.  
   *Breakdown*: Event time uses "a las".

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Ana**: "Disculpe, ¿qué hora es?" (*Excuse me, what time is it?*)
- **Luis**: "Son las nueve menos cuarto de la noche." (*It is a quarter to nine at night (8:45 PM).*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: How do you say "It is 2:30"?  
**Options**: Es la dos y media, Son las dos y media, Son las dos y treinta, Es las dos y media  
**Correct Answer**: `Son las dos y media`  
**Explanation**: Hours 2-12 take plural "Son las...".

| 2 | **Ella lee un libro interesante en la biblioteca por la tarde** | She reads an interesting book in the library in the afternoon | `Ella (Subject) + lee (Verb) + un (Object) + libro (Object) + interesante (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + biblioteca (Place) + por (Time) + la (Time) + tarde (Time)` | Uses an adjective with the object and afternoon time marker. |

### 📖 Lesson 11: Verb TENER & Idiomatic Expressions

**Subtitle**: Conjugating TENER, Physical Idioms & Tener Que  
**Professor's Note**: *"In Spanish, you don't "be" hungry or cold — you "have" hunger or cold! TENER is a fundamental verb for expressing feelings and obligations."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Master present tense conjugation of TENER (tengo, tienes, tiene...)
- Learn 8 essential physical & mental TENER idioms
- Form obligations using TENER QUE + INFINITIVE

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### Conjugation Matrix of TENER (To Have)

TENER is both a yo-go verb (tengo) and an e->ie stem changer (tienes, tiene, tienen).

| Subject | TENER Conjugation | Example Sentence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Yo | tengo | Tengo 20 años. |
| Tú | tienes | ¿Tienes hambre? |
| Él / Ella / Ud. | tiene | Él tiene frío. |
| Nosotros/as | tenemos | Tenemos prisa. |
| Vosotros/as | tenéis | Vosotros tenéis razón. |
| Ellos / Ellas / Uds. | tienen | Ellos tienen miedo. |

##### TENER Idioms & Obligation Formula

Because these idioms use nouns, modify them with MUCHO/A (a lot of) instead of MUY (very).

- Tener frío / calor = To be cold / hot (Tengo mucho frío = I am very cold).
- Tener hambre / sed = To be hungry / thirsty (Tiene mucha hambre).
- Tener prisa / miedo = To be in a hurry / afraid.
- Tener razón = To be right / correct.
- Tener X años = To be X years old.
- OBLIGATION FORMULA: TENER + QUE + INFINITIVE (e.g., Tengo que estudiar = I have to study).

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **tengo** | `TEHN-goh` | I have | TENER 1st person |
| **tienes** | `TYEH-nehs` | you have | TENER 2nd person |
| **tener hambre** | `teh-NEHR AHM-breh` | to be hungry | Idiom with noun |
| **tener sed** | `teh-NEHR SEHD` | to be thirsty | Idiom with noun |
| **tener prisa** | `teh-NEHR PREE-sah` | to be in a hurry | Idiom with noun |
| **tener que** | `teh-NEHR keh` | must / to have to | Obligation auxiliary |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `Tengo mucha sed, necesito beber agua.`  
   **English**: I am very thirsty, I need to drink water.  
   *Breakdown*: Uses mucho with noun sed.

2. **Spanish**: `Los estudiantes tienen que estudiar mucho.`  
   **English**: The students have to study a lot.  
   *Breakdown*: Obligation formula tener + que + inf.

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Mamá**: "¿Tienes hambre, Carlos?" (*Are you hungry, Carlos?*)
- **Carlos**: "Sí, tengo mucha hambre y tengo que comer ahora." (*Yes, I am very hungry and I have to eat now.*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: How do you say "I am 20 years old" in Spanish?  
**Options**: Soy 20 años, Estoy 20 años, Tengo 20 años, Hago 20 años  
**Correct Answer**: `Tengo 20 años`  
**Explanation**: Age is expressed using TENER in Spanish.

| 31 | **Yo tengo toda la razón aquí hoy** | I am completely right here today | `Yo (Subject) + tengo (Verb) + toda (Object) + la (Object) + razón (Object) + aquí (Place) + hoy (Time)` | Uses the tener razón idiom to indicate being correct. |
| 32 | **Tú tienes que estudiar en casa mañana** | You have to study at home tomorrow | `Tú (Subject) + tienes (Verb) + que (Other) + estudiar (Verb) + en (Place) + casa (Place) + mañana (Time)` | Introduces the modal structure tener que + infinitive for obligation. |
| 33 | **Ella tiene que trabajar en la oficina hoy** | She has to work at the office today | `Ella (Subject) + tiene (Verb) + que (Other) + trabajar (Verb) + en (Place) + la (Place) + oficina (Place) + hoy (Time)` | Practices third person singular obligation with tener que. |
| 34 | **Nosotros tenemos que comer en el restaurante hoy** | We have to eat at the restaurant today | `Nosotros (Subject) + tenemos (Verb) + que (Other) + comer (Verb) + en (Place) + el (Place) + restaurante (Place) + hoy (Time)` | Combines first-person plural obligation with a specific venue and time. |
| 35 | **Ellos tienen que comprar pan en el mercado ahora** | They have to buy bread at the market now | `Ellos (Subject) + tienen (Verb) + que (Other) + comprar (Verb) + pan (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + mercado (Place) + ahora (Time)` | Integrates an object noun (pan) into the tener que + infinitive construction. |
| 36 | **Pedro tiene que limpiar su dormitorio cada día** | Pedro has to clean his bedroom every day | `Pedro (Subject) + tiene (Verb) + que (Other) + limpiar (Verb) + su (Object) + dormitorio (Object) + cada (Time) + día (Time)` | Uses a possessive adjective with the object and a habitual time expression. |
| 37 | **Yo tengo hambre en la cocina a las tres** | I am hungry in the kitchen at three | `Yo (Subject) + tengo (Verb) + hambre (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + cocina (Place) + a (Time) + las (Time) + tres (Time)` | Places a specific clock time expression at the end of the sentence. |
| 38 | **Ana tiene sed en el parque por la mañana** | Ana is thirsty in the park in the morning | `Ana (Subject) + tiene (Verb) + sed (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + parque (Place) + por (Time) + la (Time) + mañana (Time)` | Combines a proper noun, a physical state idiom, a place, and a time phrase. |
| 39 | **Tú tienes frío en la calle hoy** | You are cold on the street today | `Tú (Subject) + tienes (Verb) + frío (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + calle (Place) + hoy (Time)` | Simple sentence pattern with informal second person and environment context. |
| 40 | **Nosotros tenemos calor en el jardín ahora** | We are hot in the garden now | `Nosotros (Subject) + tenemos (Verb) + calor (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + jardín (Place) + ahora (Time)` | Demonstrates the warmth/heat idiom with plural subject. |

### 📖 Lesson 12: Hacer, Weather & Saber vs Conocer

**Subtitle**: Weather Expressions with Hacer, Impersonal Expressions & Saber vs Conocer  
**Professor's Note**: *"Hacer is used for weather (Hace frío, hace sol) and Saber vs Conocer distinguishes facts/skills (Saber) from familiarity with people/places (Conocer)."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Master weather expressions using impersonal HACER (Hace frío, hace calor, hace viento)
- Distinguish between SABER (facts, information, skills) and CONOCER (people, places, acquaintance)
- Understand the personal "a" required with CONOCER when referencing specific people

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### Weather Expressions with HACER

In Spanish, we use HACER (it makes) for weather conditions rather than SER or ESTAR.

- Hace frío = It is cold / Hace calor = It is hot
- Hace sol = It is sunny / Hace viento = It is windy
- Hace buen tiempo = The weather is good / Hace mal tiempo = The weather is bad
- ¿Qué tiempo hace? = How is the weather?

##### SABER vs CONOCER Matrix

Both mean "to know", but they are never interchangeable.

| Verb | Conjugation (Yo) | Use Case | Example |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SABER | sé | Facts, information, learned skills (+ inf) | Sé hablar español. / No sé la respuesta. |
| CONOCER | conozco | People, places, pets, familiar subjects | Conozco a María. / Conozco Barcelona. |

##### The Personal "a"

When the direct object of a verb is a specific person, precede it with the preposition "a".

- Conozco a Carlos. (I know Carlos - human direct object)
- Veo a mi profesora. (I see my teacher)

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **hace frío** | `AH-seh FREE-oh` | it is cold | Weather with hacer |
| **hace sol** | `AH-seh SOHL` | it is sunny | Weather with hacer |
| **sé** | `SEH` | I know (fact/skill) | Saber 1st person |
| **conozco** | `koh-NOHZ-koh` | I know (person/place) | Conocer 1st person |
| **saber** | `sah-BEHR` | to know facts/skills | Infinitive |
| **conocer** | `koh-noh-SEHR` | to know people/places | Infinitive |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `Hoy hace mucho frío y no sé si va a llover.`  
   **English**: Today it is very cold and I don't know if it's going to rain.  
   *Breakdown*: Uses hace for weather and sé for knowing facts.

2. **Spanish**: `¿Conoces a la profesora de español? Sí, la conozco muy bien.`  
   **English**: Do you know the Spanish teacher? Yes, I know her very well.  
   *Breakdown*: Uses personal a with human object.

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Ana**: "¿Qué tiempo hace hoy en Madrid?" (*How is the weather today in Madrid?*)
- **Luis**: "Hace mucho sol pero también hace frío. ¿Sabes si el museo está abierto?" (*It is very sunny but also cold. Do you know if the museum is open?*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: Which sentence correctly says "I know Madrid very well"?  
**Options**: Sé Madrid muy bien, Conozco Madrid muy bien, Hace Madrid muy bien, Tengo Madrid muy bien  
**Correct Answer**: `Conozco Madrid muy bien`  
**Explanation**: Conocer is used for familiarity with places.

# 📚 Part 4: Irregular Verbs & Present Progressive

> **Part 4 Overview**: High-frequency stem-changing verbs (e->ie, o->ue, e->i), irregular yo-forms (gogo verbs), and active present progressive (estar + gerundio).

---

### 📖 Lesson 13: Stem-Changing Boot Verbs (Present Tense)

**Subtitle**: E->IE, O->UE, E->I & U->UE Boot Mechanics  
**Professor's Note**: *"Boot verbs change their stem vowel in all forms EXCEPT nosotros and vosotros. Visualize drawing a boot around the changing forms on a conjugation chart!"*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Understand boot verb mechanics (changes in Yo, Tú, Él, Ellos)
- Master E->IE verbs (querer, pensar, preferir)
- Master O->UE verbs (poder, almorzar, dormir) and E->I (pedir)

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### The 4 Boot Change Categories

Stem changes happen in the stressed syllable of the root.

| Category | Infinitive | Yo Form | Nosotros Form (NO CHANGE!) | They Form |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| E -> IE | querer (to want) | quiero | queremos | quieren |
| O -> UE | poder (can / to be able) | puedo | podemos | pueden |
| E -> I | pedir (to request) | pido | pedimos | piden |
| U -> UE | jugar (to play) | juego | jugamos | juegan |

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **querer (e->ie)** | `keh-REHR` | to want / to love | Boot verb |
| **poder (o->ue)** | `poh-DEHR` | to be able to / can | Boot verb |
| **pedir (e->i)** | `peh-DEER` | to ask for / order | Boot verb |
| **jugar (u->ue)** | `hoo-GAHR` | to play (sports/games) | Boot verb |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `Yo quiero comer pero nosotros preferimos estudiar.`  
   **English**: I want to eat but we prefer to study.  
   *Breakdown*: Yo changes (quiero), nosotros does not (preferimos).

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Elena**: "¿Puedes tú ir al cine hoy?" (*Can you go to the movies today?*)
- **Pablo**: "No, no puedo porque quiero dormir." (*No, I can't because I want to sleep.*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: Conjugate "poder" for "Nosotros":  
**Options**: puedemos, podemos, pidemos, puedan  
**Correct Answer**: `podemos`  
**Explanation**: Nosotros and vosotros forms NEVER undergo stem change.

#### 🧩 Interactive Sentence Builder Database (Lesson 13: 40 Exercises)

| # | Spanish Sentence | English Translation | Token Breakdown | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **Yo quiero un café caliente en la cafetería por la mañana** | I want a hot coffee at the cafeteria in the morning | `Yo (Subject) + quiero (Verb) + un (Object) + café (Object) + caliente (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + cafetería (Place) + por (Time) + la (Time) + mañana (Time)` | Demonstrates the e→ie stem-changing verb querer with a full SVO-Place-Time structure. |
| 2 | **Nosotros podemos resolver este difícil problema en la oficina hoy** | We can solve this difficult problem at the office today | `Nosotros (Subject) + podemos (Verb) + resolver (Verb) + este (Object) + difícil (Object) + problema (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + oficina (Place) + hoy (Time)` | Shows the o→ue stem-changing verb poder followed by an infinitive. |
| 3 | **Ella duerme ocho horas completas en su cama cada noche** | She sleeps eight full hours in her bed every night | `Ella (Subject) + duerme (Verb) + ocho (Object) + horas (Object) + completas (Object) + en (Place) + su (Place) + cama (Place) + cada (Time) + noche (Time)` | Illustrates the o→ue stem-changing verb dormir in the third-person singular. |
| 4 | **Tú juegas un partido emocionante en el estadio los domingos** | You play an exciting match at the stadium on Sundays | `Tú (Subject) + juegas (Verb) + un (Object) + partido (Object) + emocionante (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + estadio (Place) + los (Time) + domingos (Time)` | Practices the u→ue stem-changing verb jugar with singular 'tú'. |
| 5 | **Ellos piden una comida deliciosa en aquel restaurante por la tarde** | They order a delicious meal at that restaurant in the afternoon | `Ellos (Subject) + piden (Verb) + una (Object) + comida (Object) + deliciosa (Object) + en (Place) + aquel (Place) + restaurante (Place) + por (Time) + la (Time) + tarde (Time)` | Highlights the e→i stem-changing verb pedir in the third-person plural. |
| 6 | **Usted piensa un plan estratégico en la sala ahora mismo** | You think of a strategic plan in the living room right now | `Usted (Subject) + piensa (Verb) + un (Object) + plan (Object) + estratégico (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + sala (Place) + ahora (Time) + mismo (Time)` | Uses the formal usted pronoun with the e→ie verb pensar. |
| 7 | **Nosotros preferimos un vino tinto en esta terraza este verano** | We prefer a red wine on this terrace this summer | `Nosotros (Subject) + preferimos (Verb) + un (Object) + vino (Object) + tinto (Object) + en (Place) + esta (Place) + terraza (Place) + este (Time) + verano (Time)` | Demonstrates preferir (e→ie) with the first-person plural form (no stem change in 'nosotros'). |
| 8 | **Yo vuelvo mi viejo libro a la biblioteca a medianoche** | I return my old book to the library at midnight | `Yo (Subject) + vuelvo (Verb) + mi (Object) + viejo (Object) + libro (Object) + a (Place) + la (Place) + biblioteca (Place) + a (Time) + medianoche (Time)` | Utilizes the o→ue stem-changing verb volver in a transitive context. |
| 9 | **El camarero sirve un plato caliente en la mesa frecuentemente** | The waiter serves a hot dish at the table frequently | `El (Subject) + camarero (Subject) + sirve (Verb) + un (Object) + plato (Object) + caliente (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + mesa (Place) + frecuentemente (Time)` | Shows the e→i stem-changing verb servir with a multi-word noun subject. |

### 📖 Lesson 14: Yo-Go Verbs & Irregular Yo Forms

**Subtitle**: Pongo, Salgo, Traigo, Hago, Digo, Conozco  
**Professor's Note**: *"Some verbs are completely regular in all forms EXCEPT the "Yo" form, which adds a "-go" or "-zco"."*

#### 🎯 Learning Objectives
- Master top Yo-Go verbs (poner, salir, traer, hacer, decir, venir)
- Learn -ZCO ending verbs (conocer, conducir, ofrecer)
- Conjugate remaining forms regularly

#### 📐 Grammar & Structural Rules

##### Top Yo-Go Verbs Reference Table

| Infinitive | Yo Form (-GO) | Tú Form | Ellos Form |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| poner (to put) | pongo | pones | ponen |
| salir (to exit/go out) | salgo | sales | salen |
| traer (to bring) | traigo | traes | traen |
| hacer (to do/make) | hago | haces | hacen |
| decir (to say/tell) | digo (also e->i) | dices | dicen |
| venir (to come) | vengo (also e->ie) | vienes | vienen |

#### 🔤 Vocabulary Table

| Spanish Term | Phonetic Pronunciation | English Meaning | Usage Context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **pongo** | `POHN-goh` | I put / set | Yo-go of poner |
| **salgo** | `SAHL-goh` | I exit / go out | Yo-go of salir |
| **traigo** | `TRY-goh` | I bring | Yo-go of traer |
| **hago** | `AH-goh` | I do / make | Yo-go of hacer |

#### 💬 Core Example Sentences

1. **Spanish**: `Yo pongo los libros en la mesa y salgo con amigos.`  
   **English**: I put the books on the table and go out with friends.  
   *Breakdown*: Two yo-go verbs: pongo & salgo.

#### 🗣️ Real Dialogue Context

- **Ana**: "¿Qué haces tú los fines de semana?" (*What do you do on weekends?*)
- **Mario**: "Hago la tarea y salgo a bailar." (*I do homework and go out dancing.*)

#### ⚡ Quick Practice Check

**Question**: What is the "Yo" form of "traer"?  
**Options**: trao, traigo, trajo, trogo  
**Correct Answer**: `traigo`  
**Explanation**: Traer takes irregular yo-go form: traigo.

| # | Spanish Sentence | English Translation | Token Breakdown | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **Yo pongo mi computadora portátil sobre el escritorio limpio** | I put my laptop on the clean desk | `Yo (Subject) + pongo (Verb) + mi (Object) + computadora (Object) + portátil (Object) + sobre (Place) + el (Place) + escritorio (Place) + limpio (Place)` | Demonstrates the yo-go verb 'poner' (pongo) with a compound direct object and prepositional phrase for place. |
| 2 | **Tú sales con tus mejores amigos a la discoteca esta noche** | You go out with your best friends to the nightclub tonight | `Tú (Subject) + sales (Verb) + con (Object) + tus (Object) + mejores (Object) + amigos (Object) + a (Place) + la (Place) + discoteca (Place) + esta (Time) + noche (Time)` | Features the yo-go verb 'salir' (sales for tú) with an accompanying object phrase and time marker. |
| 3 | **Él trae un pastel de chocolate a la fiesta por la tarde** | He brings a chocolate cake to the party in the afternoon | `Él (Subject) + trae (Verb) + un (Object) + pastel (Object) + de (Object) + chocolate (Object) + a (Place) + la (Place) + fiesta (Place) + por (Time) + la (Time) + tarde (Time)` | Uses the irregular verb 'traer' (trae) followed by a complex object and a temporal adverbial phrase. |
| 4 | **Nosotros hacemos una tarea difícil en la biblioteca cada lunes** | We do difficult homework in the library every Monday | `Nosotros (Subject) + hacemos (Verb) + una (Object) + tarea (Object) + difícil (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + biblioteca (Place) + cada (Time) + lunes (Time)` | Practices the yo-go verb 'hacer' (hacemos) with a modified direct object and habitual time expression. |
| 5 | **Ellos tienen una reunión importante en la oficina central hoy** | They have an important meeting in the central office today | `Ellos (Subject) + tienen (Verb) + una (Object) + reunión (Object) + importante (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + oficina (Place) + central (Place) + hoy (Time)` | Showcases the stem-changing/yo-go verb 'tener' (tienen) paired with an object containing an adjective. |
| 6 | **Yo vengo de España a la universidad por la mañana** | I come from Spain to the university in the morning | `Yo (Subject) + vengo (Verb) + de (Place) + España (Place) + a (Place) + la (Place) + universidad (Place) + por (Time) + la (Time) + mañana (Time)` | Employs the yo-go verb 'venir' (vengo) combined with multiple place prepositions. |

| 29 | **Ella hace una tarea difícil en su dormitorio por la mañana** | She does a difficult homework assignment in her bedroom in the morning | `Ella (Subject) + hace (Verb) + una (Object) + tarea (Object) + difícil (Object) + en (Place) + su (Place) + dormitorio (Place) + por (Time) + la (Time) + mañana (Time)` | Highlights the yo-go verb 'hacer' (conjugated here in third person) with a modified direct object. |
| 30 | **Yo vengo de la universidad central con mi mochila pesada hoy** | I come from the central university with my heavy backpack today | `Yo (Subject) + vengo (Verb) + de (Place) + la (Place) + universidad (Place) + central (Place) + con (Other) + mi (Object) + mochila (Object) + pesada (Object) + hoy (Time)` | Focuses on the yo-go verb 'venir' (vengo) followed by an origin place phrase. |
| 31 | **Yo digo la verdad completa a mis padres durante la cena** | I tell the complete truth to my parents during dinner | `Yo (Subject) + digo (Verb) + la (Object) + verdad (Object) + completa (Object) + a (Other) + mis (Object) + padres (Object) + durante (Time) + la (Time) + cena (Time)` | Demonstrates the yo-go verb 'decir' (digo) with direct and indirect objects. |
| 32 | **Yo oigo una música hermosa en el jardín por la tarde** | I hear beautiful music in the garden in the afternoon | `Yo (Subject) + oigo (Verb) + una (Object) + música (Object) + hermosa (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + jardín (Place) + por (Time) + la (Time) + tarde (Time)` | Practices the irregular yo-go verb 'oír' (oigo) paired with a sensory direct object. |
| 33 | **Yo produzco arte moderno en mi estudio privado este año** | I produce modern art in my private studio this year | `Yo (Subject) + produzco (Verb) + arte (Object) + moderno (Object) + en (Place) + mi (Place) + estudio (Place) + privado (Place) + este (Time) + año (Time)` | Showcases the irregular -zco verb 'producir' (produzco) followed by a creative workspace. |
| 34 | **Tú pones tus llaves nuevas sobre la mesa esta mañana** | You put your new keys on the table this morning | `Tú (Subject) + pones (Verb) + tus (Object) + llaves (Object) + nuevas (Object) + sobre (Place) + la (Place) + mesa (Place) + esta (Time) + mañana (Time)` | Tests the informal 'tú' form of the verb 'poner' (pones) with a surface preposition. |
| 35 | **El chef trae unos platos exquisitos al comedor por la noche** | The chef brings some exquisite dishes to the dining room at night | `El (Subject) + chef (Subject) + trae (Verb) + unos (Object) + platos (Object) + exquisitos (Object) + al (Place) + comedor (Place) + por (Time) + la (Time) + noche (Time)` | Uses a noun subject with the third-person form of 'traer' (trae) and a rich object phrase. |
| 36 | **Nosotros hacemos planes ambiciosos para el proyecto durante el verano** | We make ambitious plans for the project during the summer | `Nosotros (Subject) + hacemos (Verb) + planes (Object) + ambiciosos (Object) + para (Other) + el (Object) + proyecto (Object) + durante (Time) + el (Time) + verano (Time)` | Practices the first-person plural form of 'hacer' (hacemos) with a seasonal time marker. |
| 37 | **Ellos tienen muchas dudas complejas en la clase cada tarde** | They have many complex doubts in class every afternoon | `Ellos (Subject) + tienen (Verb) + muchas (Object) + dudas (Object) + complejas (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + clase (Place) + cada (Time) + tarde (Time)` | Demonstrates the third-person plural of 'tener' (tienen) with a quantifier object. |
| 38 | **María viene de su viaje largo a casa hoy** | María comes from her long trip home today | `María (Subject) + viene (Verb) + de (Place) + su (Place) + viaje (Place) + largo (Place) + a (Place) + casa (Place) + hoy (Time)` | Combines a proper name subject with the third-person form of 'venir' (viene). |
| 39 | **Ustedes dicen discursos inspiradores en el auditorio esta tarde** | You (plural formal) give inspiring speeches in the auditorium this afternoon | `Ustedes (Subject) + dicen (Verb) + discursos (Object) + inspiradores (Object) + en (Place) + el (Place) + auditorio (Place) + esta (Time) + tarde (Time)` | Showcases the plural form 'dicen' of the yo-go verb 'decir'. |
| 40 | **Vosotros oís noticias sorprendentes en la radio por la mañana** | You all hear surprising news on the radio in the morning | `Vosotros (Subject) + oís (Verb) + noticias (Object) + sorprendentes (Object) + en (Place) + la (Place) + radio (Place) + por (Time) + la (Time) + mañana (Time)` | Practices the vosotros form 'oís' of the irregular verb 'oír'. |
